Across 100 initialization seeds per PDE, log–log slopes of the median operator error over widths 64–16 ,384 range from −0.512 to −0.469, consistent with square-root concentration; these slopes are also registered-campaign values. These five-scale fits do not establish a sharp asymptotic exponent. More fundamentally, the experiment does not certify the deterministic derivative envelopes or the explicit sufficient-width and step-size inequalities in Equation (9). The observations cover 3,000 GD steps with broad diagnostics every 100 steps, so they support finite-horizon spectral stability and training-loss contraction rather than an all-iteration proof. Finally, the optimized quantity is sampled constraint loss; we draw no PDE generalization or continuum-solution conclusion from it. Table 9 reports four of the seven trained widths; d1 ∈ { 4, 64 , 1,024 } were trained and are counted in the 770-run totals but are not summarized here. At d1 = 4 the necessary condition 

d1(d0 + 1 + d2) ≥ N fails for the heat, wave, biharmonic, Stokes, and Caputo designs. The campaign admits rank-infeasible widths, so those 50 runs are trained and counted in Table 5; their Jacobians cannot have full row rank, and we make no positivity or convergence claim for them. Finally, both runners record, per run, whether the loss is nonincreasing across saved checkpoints, but the retained 770-run and 1,080-run aggregates keep no monotonicity count. Consequently we report no checkpoint-monotonicity frequency for the 770-run or 1,080-run campaigns, and producing one would require rerunning the registered campaign; the per-iterate audit of Section L.8 is a separate reduced design recorded at every step. 

L.8 Per-iterate monotonicity 

Theorem 4.2 asserts a decrease at every iterate, whereas the campaigns above store the loss only at saved checkpoints. This audit closes that gap on a reduced design for which every step can be recorded. The network is the two-layer map of Theorem 2.1 with σ = tanh, γ = 1, d0 = d2 = 1, and per-neuron parameters drawn i.i.d. from Unif[ −1, 1] 3. The two rows are the value row ℓ1(u) = u(x⋆)and the mixed row ℓε 

> 2

= ( ℓ1 + εℓ ′

> 1

)/√1 + ε2, where ℓ′

> 1

(u) = u′(x⋆) is the derivative row, at x⋆ = 0 .35, so ε is the row-mixing parameter used in the control study. Training is full-batch gradient descent on the unscaled loss of Theorem 2.3 for 3 ,000 steps at η = 0 .2/λ max (K(0) ), with a contrast target that excites the weak direction. Widths are d1 ∈ { 256 , 1024 }, row mixings are ε ∈ { 1, 0.1, 0.01 }, and five seeds are run per cell, giving thirty runs. An iterate is scored while the loss retains double-precision significance, that is while L(θ(k)) >

10 −26 L(θ(0) ). The cutoff is necessary and not conservative: the residual is a difference of two quantities that agree to machine precision once the loss falls that far, so the recorded per-step ratios 116 quantize to small integers and carry no information. It is the same effect that the display floor of Figure 1 accommodates. Table 10: Per-iterate audit. Violations are steps with L(θ(k+1) ) > (1 + 10 −12 )L(θ(k)) among scored iterates, pooled over five seeds. The last two columns are the largest observed per-step loss ratio and the frozen-kernel prediction (1 − 2ηλ min (K(0) )) 2, reported for the first seed.                                      

> d1εViolations Scored iterates Worst ratio Predicted 256 102,845 ——256 0.1015 ,000 0.998359 0.998371 256 0.01 015 ,000 0.999983 0.999983 1,024 103,015 ——1,024 0.1015 ,000 0.998387 0.998389 1,024 0.01 015 ,000 0.999983 0.999983 Total 065 ,860

No violation occurs in any scored iterate, and in the four cells that retain a worst per-step ratio that ratio is below one, so the 10 −12 tolerance is inactive there. At ε = 1 the loss reaches the precision cutoff before step 3 ,000, so fewer iterates are scored and no worst-ratio figure is quoted; the ratios recorded past that point are cancellation artifacts rather than measurements. At the two smaller mixings the full horizon is scored and the largest per-step ratio matches the frozen-kernel factor to a relative discrepancy between 2 .8 × 10 −9 (at d1 = 1 ,024, ε = 0 .01) and 1 .2 × 10 −5 (at 

d1 = 256, ε = 0 .1), which is the sharpest agreement with a predicted rate anywhere in these experiments. Two limits. The design is two rows at one collocation point, not one of the eleven PDE benchmarks, so this audit speaks to the per-iterate claim and not to coverage. And the agreement with (1 − 2ηλ min )2 is agreement with the frozen initial kernel, not with the certified constants of Equation (9); it is consistent with the kernel drift reported above being small at these widths, and is not a verification of the theorem’s sufficient constants. The run is the per-iterate monotonicity experiment of the accompanying code, under its tracked configuration. 

L.9 Reproduction 

The tracked aggregate of the control study and the plotting script in the accompanying code reproduce Figure 1 with one command. The script validates the displayed counts and five-seed medians and records the hash of the original 1,080-row source table. Summary statistics cannot reconstruct that source table. The prepare and submit commands documented with the code rerun the full campaign through its campaign driver. After such a run, the control aggregation script selects and aggregates compatible controls, while the evidence-assessment script produces the operator-class, width, Jacobian-drift, neuron-motion, frozen-dynamics, and rank-tolerance summaries. The current repository does not contain the original per-run 770-PDE, 1,080-control, or 7,700-initialization records; consequently, recomputing those broad-study summaries requires rerunning the registered campaign.
